# Decisions

What we decided and briefly why. Numbers are stable: append new decisions at the end, mark
superseded ones instead of deleting them. Paths are the current ones.

| # | Topic | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Engine | WebKit at a WPE release, own `PORT=Wasm` | Of the big engines WebKit ports best (CLoop, curl backend, generic RunLoop) |
| 2 | Process model | Single process in a Web Worker | Rebuilding WebKit2 IPC in WASM would be a lot of work and slower (copies instead of shared memory) |
| 3 | Port base | Structure like the PlayStation port, graphics like WPE (Skia) | PlayStation = curl + RunLoopGeneric + static, without GLib/libsoup. A slimmed WPE would have to cut out GLib, soup and WebKit2 |
| 4 | JS | JSC CLoop, no JIT | WASM cannot run a normal JIT. CLoop is an official, tested mode |
| 5 | Memory model | wasm32 (no Memory64) | Memory64 is clearly slower. 4 GB are enough with trimmed features |
| 6 | Graphics | Skia, CPU first, then WebGL 2 | WebKit no longer supports Cairo. Skia is proven in WASM (CanvasKit) |
| 7 | Network | Wisp over WebSocket to our own relay | Browsers have no raw TCP/UDP, `fetch()` fails on CORS. Wisp carries TCP and UDP (WireGuard later) |
| 8 | TLS | OpenSSL inside WASM | End-to-end encrypted, the relay sees no content. The WebCore curl backend needs the OpenSSL API |
| 9 | Threads | Emscripten pthreads, no Asyncify | Asyncify makes the code much bigger. Maybe JSPI later |
| 10 | Target browsers | Chrome/Chromebook first, then Firefox, Safari optional | Project goal; Chrome has the best WASM support |
| 11 | Fetching WebKit | Script with shallow + sparse + blobless fetch instead of a submodule | A submodule pulls GBs of tests. This way about 300 MB |
| 12 | Build | GitHub Actions with a Docker toolchain image | Public repo, same environment locally and in CI |
| 13 | Reference | WebkitWasm (theogbob) as reference only, no fork | We want a clean, reproducible base. BSD-2-Clause, taking code is allowed with the copyright notice ([licensing.md](licensing.md)) |
| 14 | License | BSD-2-Clause for our own code | Compatible with WebKit (LGPL/BSD) and the reference |
| 15 | Meson | From pip (pinned) instead of the Ubuntu package | fontconfig 2.17 needs Meson >= 1.6.1, Ubuntu 24.04 has 1.3 |
| 16 | ICU data | Built ourselves from the host archive as `libicudata.a` | In a cross build ICU builds only stub data |
| 17 | GC roots (superseded by #36) | `--spill-pointers` for the whole binary | Without it live objects get lost (proven with a stress test) |
| 18 | First JSC build | `PORT=JSCOnly` with patches, own `PORT=Wasm` only for WebCore | JSCOnly is enough for JSC and keeps the patches small |
| 19 | Spill and names | `--spill-pointers` needs `--profiling-funcs` | Without names the pass cannot find the stack pointer after full optimization |
| 20 | Link flags of jsc | Only on the target `jsc`, not global | The LLInt extractors (linked with -O0) do not survive `--spill-pointers` |
| 21 | Theme | Adwaita (like the Windows port) | Ready form controls and scrollbars without dependencies |
| 22 | Clients | Make the empty clients overridable by patch (`final` -> `override`) | Mechanical patch; our clients override only what is needed |
| 23 | Event loop | Pump the RunLoop from outside (wake-up callback + timer for the next WebCore timer) | A worker must not block, otherwise no messages arrive |
| 24 | Frames to the host (superseded by #83) | Pixels in shared memory, host copies with putImageData, one frame in flight | Simple and robust until the GPU path |
| 25 | UI (superseded by #199) | Plain HTML/CSS/JS without a build step instead of TypeScript + Vite | No npm dependencies, easier hosting, enough for the UI |
| 26 | Compositing (superseded by #94) | No accelerated compositing, empty `GraphicsLayerWasm` | Everything paints into the pixel buffer; enough until the GPU path |
| 27 | Exceptions | Allow throwing at link time, not catching | Dependencies (C++) reference `__cxa_throw`. A throw aborts, as WebKit expects |
| 28 | CA certificates (superseded by #162) | Mozilla bundle in the data package, optional `extra-ca.crt` | Company networks with their own CA, local tests behind a TLS proxy |
| 29 | ICU data | Filter with `icupkg -r` instead of rebuilding from source | The source archive has only the finished `.dat`. Filtering converters and languages saves 14 MB |
| 30 | Delivery | Engine per version in its own folder, cached immutable, precompressed `.br` | Never an old .wasm from a cache; Brotli q11 once at deploy instead of per request |
| 31 | Text input | Hidden `<textarea>` instead of focus on the canvas | Only this way dead keys, IME and on-screen keyboards arrive as finished text |
| 32 | Popups | The engine opens the view itself (ids from 2^20), the UI makes a tab of it | `createWindow` must return a Page synchronously |
| 33 | Downloads (superseded by #163 and #412) | Load the file again with an own CurlRequest, data to the UI | Simpler than rehanging the running load; the URL is already the target after redirects |
| 34 | Storage (superseded by #142) | IDBFS (JS file system) instead of OPFS/WasmFS | WasmFS had no sockets and our network used SOCKFS (no longer since #68) |
| 35 | localStorage/IndexedDB | Build the files of `WebKitLegacy/Storage` | Finished, tested implementation (InProcessIDBServer, StorageAreaSync) |
| 36 | GC roots (engine) | GC only on an empty stack (DeferGC around every task, patch 0012) instead of `--spill-pointers` | The pass made everything about 10x slower and the .wasm 34 MB bigger. Covered by a stress test (crash without deferral) |
| 37 | Fonts | Generate the fontconfig cache at build time, font folders get mtime 0 | fontconfig then trusts the cache and does not rescan the fonts at every start |
| 38 | Missing Content-Type | Sniff like other browsers (extension, else HTML) | Otherwise the page was treated as a download |
| 39 | WebSockets | `WebSocketChannel` + `SocketStreamHandle` copied from WebKitLegacy (`engine/src/websocket/`), own socket on WebCore's `CurlStreamScheduler` | WebCore has no single-process WebSocket channel any more; WebKitLegacy only a CFNetwork one |
| 40 | Video | `ENABLE_VIDEO` on (playback see #46) | Pages expect the media API (`video.pause()` etc.) |
| 41 | Web Audio | The engine renders on its own thread into a ring in WASM memory, an AudioWorklet of the host plays it | No copies over postMessage, no lock; the worklet reads shared memory directly |
| 42 | Audio decoder | `decodeAudioData` through an `OfflineAudioContext` of the host | All formats of the browser, without ffmpeg in WASM |
| 43 | FFT | Own small radix-2 FFT instead of a library | WebCore otherwise only has vDSP (Apple) or GStreamer; enough for Web Audio sizes |
| 44 | Favicons | The engine loads the icon and sends the bytes to the host | The host has no network of its own (everything goes through the relay); cookies come along |
| 45 | Fullscreen | The whole view area goes fullscreen, not a single element | The engine paints everything into one canvas; WebCore lays the element over the page itself |
| 46 | Video playback | A host `<video>` plays, the engine paints the copied frames | All codecs of the browser, sound directly. Copying frames costs some CPU, but overlays, clipping and scrolling are exact |
| 47 | Video data | A service worker asks the engine in chunks (Range, 2 MB) | The host has no network; the next chunk comes only when the browser reads, so memory stays small |
| 48 | MSE | WebCore MSE with own parsers (timestamps only), data also to a MediaSource of the host | `buffered`, seeking and events follow the standard, decoding stays with the browser |
| 49 | Aligned memory | WTF rounds the size of `aligned_alloc` up to a multiple of the alignment (patch 0015) | Emscripten returns nullptr otherwise (C11 rule) and WebKit crashes, for example loading HRTF |
| 50 | WebKit bug in `VectorMath::multiplyComplex` | Store the result in a temporary first (patch 0013) | The non-SIMD code overwrote the real part too early, FFT convolution (ConvolverNode, HRTF) was wrong. Hidden on x86/ARM where SIMD computes |
| 51 | WebGL | No ANGLE in WASM, GL calls go to Emscripten's GLES (host WebGL in the engine worker) | ANGLE in WASM would be huge and end up calling WebGL anyway. The browser validates shaders and limits itself |
| 52 | WebGL version | A WebGL 1 page gets a WebGL 1 host context, WebGL 2 a WebGL 2 context | Without ANGLE's shader translator the shader language must match exactly |
| 53 | ANGLE functions | Generated from the Khronos headers by `tools/gen-angle-shim.py`, the rest by hand | About 300 functions, nearly all 1:1. Regenerate on a WebKit update |
| 54 | Many WebGL contexts | One host context per WebGL version, state saved and restored on switch | Chrome allows only 4 contexts per worker. Fingerprinting and feature tests create short-lived contexts that would kill the page's |
| 55 | Image encoders | Skia encoders on (PNG, JPEG, WebP) | Without them `toDataURL`/`toBlob` always gave `data:,`. The libs were in the sysroot already |
| 56 | WebAssembly of the pages | Modules run in the browser's WASM engine, API rebuilt in JSC | JSC cannot run WebAssembly without JIT. An interpreter in WASM would be 20-50x slower |
| 57 | Module memory | Rewrite the module: its memory is a region of the engine memory, every access bounds-checked | JSC must show `memory.buffer` as a normal ArrayBuffer inside engine memory. The check keeps each module in its region |
| 58 | Base and size in code | In locals per function, reloaded after calls | Reading imported globals at every access was about 3x slower, this costs about 12 % |
| 59 | WebAssembly API | Evaluated on first access (getter on the global object) | Most pages never use WebAssembly and pay nothing |
| 60 | GC during long scripts | Own VM trap, collected only in the interpreter at `op_check_traps` and only without nested C++ | There all values are in memory, not in WASM locals. First page: peak 443 instead of 531 MB. `--spill-pointers` would be 14x slower again |
| 61 | Releasing memory | `WebCore::releaseMemory` automatically from 192 MB used, not only on memory pressure | WASM memory never shrinks and the browser reports no memory pressure. Costs about 0.3 s, so at most every 5 s and after 64 MB growth (trigger see #64) |
| 62 | Call caches in the interpreter | `forceICFailure` off on WASM (patch 0019) | On 32 bit it is on by default but only matters for JIT code we do not have. The interpreter caches work on 32 bit; without them every call detoured through C++ |
| 63 | Skia on WASM | SSE4.1 path through Emscripten's SSE mapping to WASM SIMD (patch 0020) | The scalar pipeline paints one pixel per step. SSE4.1 halves paint time; an own WASM SIMD path would be much more work |
| 64 | Memory release trigger | Growth of `malloc_footprint()` instead of used memory | Free to read and hits exactly the moment WASM memory would grow |
| 65 | TLS ciphers | ChaCha20-Poly1305 before AES-GCM, also for TLS 1.3 | Without AES hardware ChaCha20 is about 4x faster in software. WebKit only set the TLS 1.2 list, TLS 1.3 used OpenSSL's default (AES-256-GCM first) |
| 66 | WebCrypto | Fix the OpenSSL 3 bugs in WebKit's code instead of an own implementation | Only two small places (AES-GCM order, RSA macro checks). AWS WAF checks (SBB, IMDb, Booking) pass |
| 67 | SQLite files | Own VFS in WASM memory, whole files written (since #142 to OPFS on the engine thread, replaced atomically; since #180 only the database with the WAL folded in) | Every file access went synchronously through the main thread (Emscripten's JS file system) |
| 68 | Sockets | Own layer in WASM (`--wrap` at link time) instead of Emscripten's SOCKFS | No synchronous detour through the main thread for every send/recv/poll. Transport stays replaceable (WireGuard). No curl patch |
| 69 | Main thread and locks | Nothing that can wait under a lock on the main thread | While waiting on the main thread Emscripten runs its queue; that caused self deadlocks (nzz.ch, 20min.ch hung now and then) |
| 70 | HTTP cache | Cache API of the host instead of files in IDBFS. Counts only its own content (`x-bl-size`), limit 400 MB and at most a quarter of the quota, trimmed to 80 % beyond. HTTP/1 responses that end only with the connection are not stored. Lookup and revalidation for all loaders see #182 | IDBFS loaded everything into memory at start and wrote the whole tree every 5 s. The Cache API stays on disk and is read on demand |
| 71 | WebKit bug in the C loop stack limit | `StackManager::cancelStop` resets the JS stack limit in the C loop, not the native one (patch 0022) | After the first VM trap the interpreter checked against the wrong limit; deep recursion wrote past the JS stack (crash on x.com, broken GC mark stack). Test: `tests/engine/pages/stack.html` |
| 72 | GC during long scripts (addendum to #60) | Also collect at function entry (slow path of the stack check), not only at `op_check_traps` | A trap sets the stack limit so the next call lands in the slow path, where the trap was dropped. On real pages the GC almost never ran (CNN: 0 instead of 6 times) |
| 73 | Ed25519/X25519 in WebCrypto | Own OpenSSL code (`EVP_PKEY_ED25519/X25519`, patch 0010) | WebKit only has Apple and libgcrypt code. With EVP it is three small files; OpenSSL is there for TLS anyway |
| 74 | Virtual memory under Emscripten | `tryLargeZeroedMemalignVirtual` and `OSAllocator::zeroFill` with `aligned_alloc`/`memset` (patch 0023) | Emscripten cannot release parts of a mapping and has no `MAP_FIXED`. Resizable ArrayBuffer crashed (`new ArrayBuffer(n, {maxByteLength})`) |
| 75 | WebCore settings | Enable everything WebKit browsers (WPE/GTK/Safari) enable that needs no backend of its own | Defaults for "WebCore alone" are often `false`. About 30 finished features came in (Navigation API, date/color inputs, Compression Streams ...) |
| 76 | Pickers (`<select>`, color, date) | `<select>` as an own list in the UI, color/date through a hidden host field with `showPicker()` | The list must fit keyboard and window; for color/date the real system dialog beats a replica. Now `@browser-lite/ui` |
| 77 | Yardstick | A page with feature checks runs in the host Chromium and in browser-lite, differences as a list, a stored list guards against regressions | Shows objectively how close we are to Chromium and what is missing next |
| 78 | Feature switches | Drag support, touch, gamepad, notifications, geolocation, pointer lock, OffscreenCanvas, variable fonts, Media Session, speech synthesis on at once | Every switch rebuilds all of WebKit; the host binding lives in `engine/src` (fast to build) |
| 79 | Permissions (storage superseded by #206) | Own prompt in the UI, decision per origin of the top page (notifications per own origin). `camera-microphone` covers both, `permissions.query` returns the remembered decision, a tab closed with an open question remembers nothing | Like Chrome. The host browser asks for browser-lite only once (location, notifications for the whole app) |
| 80 | Touch | Touch events to the page, scrolling/click only when the page does not call `preventDefault` | Maps and games with own touch logic work, normal pages scroll as usual |
| 81 | `ontouchstart` | Only when the host reports a touchscreen (`navigator.maxTouchPoints`) | WebKit otherwise always says "touch" (except GTK) and many pages show the mobile view |
| 82 | Session | Background tabs load on first click | With the interpreter every page costs a lot of time and memory. Chrome does the same |
| 83 | Showing frames | Each view canvas goes with `transferControlToOffscreen` to the engine's worker, the engine paints into it | The main thread copied every frame and waited up to 1.7 s for the compositor, and every engine call through the main thread waited too. CNN: engine waited 0.5 instead of 4.5 s, load 19.8 -> 16.2 s |
| 84 | Waiting for the main thread | Remember answers instead of asking again: Skia font checks, `canPlayType`, `access()` on fixed files; one shared crypto queue (patch 0024) | Skia read whole font files through the JS file system on every lookup, every iframe started its own thread for `window.crypto`. 3.5 s waiting on CNN |
| 85 | Allocator | dlmalloc stays, mimalloc tested | mimalloc was slower (CNN hit the time limit). dlmalloc's lock costs only about 0.2 s while loading |
| 86 | GPU raster | Skia (Ganesh) paints through WebGL 2 in the engine worker, one shared context, frame via `transferToImageBitmap`. On by default, `engine.gpu: false` turns it off; without WebGL 2 or after context loss CPU (back to GPU after restore, #157) | Gradients, scaled images, shadows and text cost almost nothing on the engine thread. Even with SwiftShader scrolling: NZZ 1226 -> 481 ms, GitHub longest frame 481 -> 184 ms |
| 87 | Skia GL context | Every Skia GL call first checks that its context is current (wrapper in Skia's WebGL interface, patch 0020) | Skia calls GL while painting, and WebGL canvases switch the context mid-paint. Switching only on flush gave white WebGL frames and a crash on CNN |
| 88 | Engine thread tasks | Own list instead of Emscripten's queue; a pass runs only what was queued at its start, then back to the event loop (MessageChannel) | Emscripten drains the queue while more arrives, but an OffscreenCanvas frame only appears at the end of the JS task: GitHub stayed white 10 s, now 5 s and updating |
| 89 | More web APIs | Canvas filters, `moveBefore`, `select.showPicker`, `scrollbar-color`, Storage API, Screen Orientation, Wake Lock by setting; own StorageProvider and ScreenOrientationManager (`engine/src/WebPlatformExtras.cpp`) | Finished in WebKit, only the browser side was missing. Yardstick 217 -> 228 of 277 |
| 90 | Polyfills | `scheduler.postTask/yield` (+ `TaskController`) and `navigator.vibrate` as a user script before every page script | Chromium has them, WebKit not. Only APIs with clear meaning that can be rebuilt cleanly; no Chrome identity (`userAgentData`; `connection` and `deviceMemory` yes, with host values, #120) |
| 91 | Links in a new tab | Ctrl/Cmd+click and middle click caught in the engine's navigation policy, background tab like Chrome | WebKit reports both as a link click with button/modifiers |
| 92 | View source (shown as a page by #490) | The engine loads the address like a download (same cookies), the UI shows the text with line numbers | WebKit has no view-source mode; this shows exactly what the server sent |
| 93 | Cache API | Own `CacheStorageConnection` in the engine for all views, separated by origin (`engine/src/CacheStorageWasm.cpp`); persistent since #105, bodies as files since #155 | WebKit implements it only in the WebKit2 network process |
| 94 | Compositing with layers | WebKit's Coordinated Graphics like WPE (GraphicsLayerCoordinated, SkiaPaintingEngine, SkiaCompositingLayer), since #98 on an own compositor thread (`engine/src/LayerTreeHostWasm.cpp`). On when WebGL 2 works, `engine.compositing: false` turns it off, after GPU loss no layers | SwiftShader: NZZ scrolling 6 slow frames -> 0, GitHub 1946 -> 701 ms; loading CNN 17.2 -> 14.5 s. `transform`/`opacity` animations, `will-change`, fixed elements and scrolling only composite. Tiles paint on CPU threads, CSS filters on the GPU. Patch 0025 adapts the EGL parts to WebGL (RGBA, context before each call) |
| 95 | Origin Private File System | Own `FileSystemStorageConnection` on the engine thread (`engine/src/FileSystemStorageWasm.cpp`), files under `/data/opfs/<origin>/` | WebKit2 has it in the network process. Same rules (names, locks, errors); `createWritable` writes a copy under `/data/opfs-swap`, `close()` renames it over the file, leftovers are deleted at start; `move()` fails while source or target is locked |
| 96 | `requestVideoFrameCallback` | Setting on, `MediaPlayerPrivateWasm` reports size, media time and frame number per new frame | Chromium has it, video sites use it for subtitles and effects |
| 97 | Orientation/motion sensors (completed by #102) | Clients and host events ready, the WebKit switch comes with the next full rebuild | A switch in `OptionsWasm.cmake` does not change the local CMake cache; untested it stays out of CI |
| 98 | Compositor thread | Own pthread with its own WebGL 2 context composites and presents; the view canvases belong to its worker (`engine/src/Compositor.cpp`). The engine thread does rendering update and flush, the compositor takes over at its worker's next rAF | CSS animations keep running while page JS blocks the engine thread. Basis for async scrolling |
| 99 | GL objects and threads | BitmapTexture deletes textures/framebuffers through the engine (`bl_texmap_gl_delete`), which forwards to the context's thread | WebGL ids are valid only in the worker that made them; deleting on the engine thread would hit a foreign texture with the same number |
| 100 | GPU loss with layers | The engine ends compositing in all frames itself (`clearBackingForAllLayers`, `enableCompositingMode(false)`) | The setting alone acts only at the next style/layout change; the page stayed white until then |
| 101 | Async scrolling | WebKit's scrolling tree like WPE (`ENABLE_ASYNC_SCROLLING`, `SCROLLING_THREAD`, `KINETIC_SCROLLING`, `WHEEL_EVENT_REGIONS`). Wheel and swipe events go from the host through the compositor thread to WebCore's scrolling thread (`engine/src/ScrollingWasm.cpp`); the engine thread gets them only where the page has a blocking `wheel` listener. `BL_ASYNC_SCROLLING=0` turns it off | Scrolling stays smooth while page JS blocks (test: 5 s endless loop, the page still scrolls). The compositor sends a display tick after each wheel event and during smooth scrolling because WebKit applies scrolling thread positions only at the next display refresh. That tick counts as wheel activity even when it comes later than the 50 ms WebKit allows for it (slow machine), otherwise the position waited for the engine thread |
| 102 | Orientation/motion sensors (addendum to #97) | `ENABLE_DEVICE_ORIENTATION` on, with #101 in one full rebuild | `deviceorientation`/`devicemotion` of the host reach the visible view while the page listens |
| 103 | Service workers and SharedWorker | WebCore's SWServer, SWClientConnection and SWContextManager on the engine thread, connected without IPC but always through the RunLoop (`engine/src/ServiceWorkerWasm.cpp`). Fetch interception in our LoaderStrategy. SharedWorker like WebSharedWorkerServer. Soft update like ServiceWorkerSoftUpdateLoader, patch 0033 lets the SWServer recognize its own jobs. `WindowClient.navigate()` ends with the frame load. Preload and `openWindow` see #154 | Many sites and all PWAs use service workers. WebCore has everything, only the process connections were missing. Not yet: Push, Background Fetch. `WindowClient.focus()` see #169 |
| 104 | Background image decoding | One shared thread for all images instead of a WorkQueue per image (patch 0024) | Each new thread needs a Web Worker with the whole WASM module and the caller waits for it. A bigger pool (16) brought nothing measurable, so 8 stays |
| 105 | Persistent Cache API and service workers | Cache API: one file per origin under `/data/cachestorage` in WebKit's CacheStorageDiskStore format, at most 256 MB per origin (`put`/`addAll` all or nothing); bodies see #155. Service workers: WebCore's `SWRegistrationDatabase` under `/data/serviceworkers`; navigations wait for the import of their origin's registrations like WebKit2 | PWAs work offline again after a browser restart. Clearing data empties `/data` and with it this |
| 106 | Polyfill bridge | Hidden function `__blHost` that only the polyfill script sees (deleted before the first page script). The host runs a fixed list of host APIs (now the services of `@browser-lite/core`): EyeDropper, `navigator.getBattery`, `navigator.keyboard` | WebKit lacks these APIs, Chromium has them. The page is a canvas in the host, so the host's eyedropper works directly. Yardstick 239 of 277 |
| 107 | WebCodecs | `ENABLE_WEB_CODECS` on. Each page codec gets a real host codec in the engine worker (`engine/src/media/WebCodecsWasm.cpp`, `engine/js/webcodecs-host.js`); calls from workers go to the engine thread. Own `VideoFrame` with pixels in engine memory (I420, I420A, I422, I444, NV12, RGBA/BGRA), YUV -> RGB by color matrix and range; patch 0026 removes WebCore's empty placeholders | Video editors, conferencing and games use WebCodecs. The host codecs cover everything Chrome can (VP8/VP9/AV1/H.264, Opus/AAC/FLAC) without codecs in WASM. Yardstick 241 of 277 |
| 108 | Speech recognition | `webkitSpeechRecognition` with an own `SpeechRecognitionProvider` (`engine/src/DeviceClients.cpp`): the engine asks for the microphone per origin like Chrome, the host recognizes with its `SpeechRecognition` and reports back | Dictation and voice search work. WebKit has the API, only the recognizer was missing. Yardstick 242 of 277 |
| 109 | Printing | WebCore's `PrintContext` breaks the page with `@media print` into A4 pages (1 cm margin, layout in CSS px like Chrome), Skia's PDF backend makes a PDF (`engine/src/Printing.cpp`, like WebKitGTK); the view gets a `print` event. An almost empty last page is dropped | The host's print dialog prints real pages instead of a canvas screenshot. `beforeprint`/`afterprint` like Chrome, same page count as Chrome in the test |
| 110 | LTO | No `-flto=thin` | Built all of WebKit with ThinLTO and measured: CNN, Wikipedia and GitHub load equally fast (16.5-17.7 s, 5.5-6.4 s, 2.5-2.9 s), the micro benchmark (JS, DOM, parser, style) is equal or worse, the .wasm 8 MB bigger. While loading the engine waits only about 0.2 s for new threads (WorkQueues), the rest is computation in WebCore and JSC |
| 111 | Sound in the tab | The engine reports `isPlayingMediaDidChange` (Web Audio and media elements), views can be muted (`Page::setMuted`) | Like Chrome: see which tab plays sound and mute it without leaving the page |
| 112 | File System Access | `showOpenFilePicker`, `showSaveFilePicker`, `showDirectoryPicker` as a polyfill over the host bridge: the real Chrome handles stay in the host, the page gets objects with the prototypes of WebKit's handles and only a random id, valid only for the origin that got it. Contents go as Base64; a dropped open writable is aborted by a `FinalizationRegistry`; handles belong to their view | Web apps like vscode.dev, Excalidraw and Squoosh open and save real files like in Chrome |
| 113 | Camera, microphone, screen | `ENABLE_MEDIA_STREAM` (and `MEDIA_RECORDER`) on. Own factories, devices and sources (`engine/src/media/MediaStreamWasm.cpp`, patch 0027): a starting source opens the host device and hands the `ReadableStream` of a `MediaStreamTrackProcessor` directly to the engine worker; frames become `VideoFrameWasm`, sound goes as Float32 to WebCore. Prompt per origin like Chrome, device names only after permission. `srcObject` shows the newest frame, Web Audio gets the microphone through an own `AudioSourceProvider` | Video chats, QR scanners, dictation, screen recording with WebCodecs. No pixel goes through the main thread. Yardstick 245 of 277 |
| 114 | MediaRecorder | Own `MediaRecorderPrivate` (`engine/src/media/MediaRecorderWasm.cpp`, patch 0027). Frames and sound go raw to the engine worker, the host's `VideoEncoder`/`AudioEncoder` encode (VP8, VP9, AV1, Opus) and a small WebM writer in JS (`engine/js/mediarecorder-host.js`) muxes like Chrome. Types like Chrome, no MP4. `MediaStreamAudioDestinationNode` now delivers sound | The host `MediaRecorder` does not exist in workers and a detour through the main thread costs more. WebCodecs is there already. Yardstick 246 of 277 |
| 115 | Cheaper WebGL context switches | All WebGL contexts share a host context (patch 0016). Extensions and limits are queried once per host context, uniform buffers and constant vertex attributes are read only when set since the last restore. Switch WebGL 2: 32 -> 8.6 ms, new context 33 -> 9.6 ms | Pages with several canvases and fingerprint scripts blocked the engine. No queries at all would need a full shadow copy of all GL setters, too error prone |
| 116 | Known: forced layout in nested flexboxes | On 20min.ch changing `span.style` and reading `offsetLeft` costs 45-52 ms (Chromium 0.07 ms): WebKit relayouts flex items with percentage heights on every container layout (FIXME in WebKit). Chromium has a layout cache | Not changed: an own cache would also have to cover stretching and could bring layout bugs we cannot see without WebKit's layout tests. Safari behaves the same |
| 117 | WebRTC through the host (stage 1: data channels) | Instead of libwebrtc a polyfill (`engine/src/WebRTCPolyfill.cpp`): `RTCPeerConnection`, `RTCDataChannel` and friends with Chrome's API. Every connection has a real counterpart in the host (`packages/core/src/services/webrtc.ts`); events by long polling, states mirrored with sequence numbers. Connections belong to their origin, orphans close after 60 s | File transfer, games, WebTorrent work, also with normal Chrome peers. The network is the host's, not the relay (peers see the device IP), hence opt-in (#204) |
| 118 | WebRTC stage 2: media | `addTrack`, `addTransceiver`, `removeTrack`, senders, receivers, transceivers (`replaceTrack`, `setParameters`, `direction`, `stop`, `getStats`), `ontrack` with streams as in the SDP. Sending: camera and microphone go out as a copy of the real host track (no detour through the engine); other tracks (canvas, Web Audio, video) are written by an engine sink into a host `MediaStreamTrackGenerator` (WritableStream straight to the engine worker). Receiving: the peer's track flows through a `MediaStreamTrackProcessor` into a new engine source, the page gets a normal `MediaStreamTrack` (hidden media bridge only for the polyfill). Chrome delivers WebRTC sound only while it is played: a muted host `<audio>` keeps it flowing | Video calls (Meet, Jitsi, Discord) need exactly this. Tested both ways against a real Chromium page. The host swaps the SDP ids (`a=msid`) for the page's (back on `setLocalDescription`), so the peer sees the page's stream id like in Chrome. Remote tracks are muted while the host reports them muted. On `close()` host tracks are released, the engine sink stays for a later `addTrack`; it goes with `stop()` or when its source ends. The polyfill holds sent tracks strongly: held only weakly, the track was destroyed in a GC, ended its source in the middle of the sweep and the engine crashed (webrtc-test, camera part) |

Whole files (SQLite, Cache API) are replaced atomically by `replaceWholeFile`: copy in `/data/tmp`, then `rename`; `/data/tmp` is emptied at start, a read error never counts as "file missing". Without OPFS (node) `/data` stays in memory. Measured, 3 runs each: waiting for the UI thread on the engine thread CNN 0.61 -> 0.08 s, NZZ 0.16 -> 0.03 s, Wikipedia 0.30 -> 0.01 s; load time CNN 25.0 -> 24.2 s, NZZ 21.4 -> 21.0 s, Wikipedia 15.4 -> 15.2 s |

Workers so use their storage key: service and shared workers load through the placeholder document of their page (its `firstPartyForCookies` is the top origin of the registration or creator, its own origin is opaque, so the frame's document is not used), dedicated workers through the creator's document; the background update takes the partition of the registration, the navigation preload that of the navigation. WebCore gives a CORS load after a cross-origin redirect an opaque origin; then the frame's context decides (the worker origin registered for a worker page, else the document), so such loads stay cached. Opaque contexts (sandboxed frames, `data:` tops) and origins without a site (`file:`) are not cached. Entry URL `https://http.invalid/?p=<partition>&u=<url>` in `<ns>-http-v2`; the bytecode key is `<partition> <url>`. Opening deletes the other formats of the cache name, `browser.storage.clearCaches()` all of them (`engine/js/http-cache.js`); no migration. | 222 | Ranges (206) in the HTTP cache (U8b) | Like Chrome's sparse entries, as far as it stays simple. A 206 to a request with one range from a position on (`bytes=a-b`, `bytes=a-`) is stored as a range of its resource when it has a strong validator (a strong ETag, or a Last-Modified at least 60 s before the Date, like Chrome's `HasStrongValidators`), a `Content-Range` that starts at the requested byte and names the total (at most 10 MB, the limit of a whole entry), no `Content-Encoding` and a body as long as the range. Key: that of the resource plus `&r=<first>-<last>/<total>&v=<version>` (version: ETag, Last-Modified and Vary values); the Cache API refuses 206, so a range is stored as 200. A range request is answered once the ranges of the newest version cover the whole resource: `http-cache.js` stitches the requested bytes (overlaps allowed) into a 206 with the headers of the newest range, whose freshness and validator decide as for a whole entry; Vary and the partition apply as for whole responses. Storing a range deletes the ranges of other versions and those inside it, storing a whole response all ranges of its resource. A 304 updates only the headers of the stored entry (for ranges the newest range of the version), for every entry: the body stays in the Cache API instead of going through the engine and the main thread again, as Chrome only updates the headers. `MediaFetch` (the pieces of the host `<video>`) looks up, revalidates and stores through the cache in the partition of the element's document; `HttpCache::Writer` writes responses along for it and for `ResourceLoad`; a redirect is followed without the validators of the entry looked up, which belong to the first URL only (as `ResourceLoad` follows from the loader's request). `http-cache.js` keeps the stored ranges per resource next to its list of keys, so storing and looking up does not depend on the size of the cache. Not taken: answering from partly stored ranges and loading only the gaps (Chrome does this with `If-Range`); without it the pieces of one playback never mix two versions, and media worth caching (loops of 1 to 3 MB) is loaded whole anyway. Not taken: ranges from a whole 200 entry, a whole request from ranges (rare; one kind of entry per kind of request keeps revalidation simple). Media after a redirect is not stored. Measuring: Web Page Replay sent the whole archived response to every conditional request, so savings by 304 never showed; `tests/perf/wpr-conditional.patch` answers a request whose validator matches the archived response with 304 like the origin (media.cnn.com answers a conditional range request with 304, checked live) | CNN loads loop videos (`media.cnn.com/api/v1/loops/...mp4`, 1 to 3 MB) in pieces of 2 MB, about three quarters of the bytes of a visit; they have no ETag but a strong Last-Modified, `max-age=300` and an `Age` of hours, so they arrive stale and revalidate on every visit. CI perf run 39755904376 (plan `range.json`, 30 runs per arm in ABBA order, 480 runs, all valid, no crash; base main a54a5e1 from CI 39718841284, arm CI 39751406864; the job S CNN ran again after a 503 of the artifact download). Second visit of CNN, C mobile: rx G 0.415 [0.281, 0.635] (14.4 to 6.0 MB), CPU 0.873 [0.771, 1.003], first layout 0.978 [0.940, 1.000], loaded 0.881 (secondary); S LAN: rx 0.062 [0.061, 0.063] (15.0 to 0.93 MB), CPU 0.714 [0.666, 0.746], first layout 0.999 [0.939, 1.043] (medians 962 and 961 ms), loaded 0.725. C saves less because the replay moves Last-Modified with the time of each response (`updateDates` of Web Page Replay): two pieces loaded in different seconds count as two versions there, which happens more often on the slow device; real servers keep it. YouTube (no ranged traffic beyond 26 KB of sounds, `videoplayback` fails in the replay): rx 1.000, CPU 0.995 (C) and 1.005 (S), first layout 0.986 (C). First visit: CNN rx 0.817 (C) and 0.672 (S), as loops requested again during the visit now hit, CPU 0.954 and 0.935; YouTube within 1 %. Memory (anon) at most 1.006. Rule correction passes for YouTube and the first visit. Rule lever (`stats.mjs`): C wins clearly, every bound on S is below 1.03 except first layout at an unchanged median, whose interval is as wide as its noise on this page (S CNN first layout in three runs: 1.025 [0.959, 1.084], 0.991 [0.972, 1.037], 0.999 [0.939, 1.043]), so the tool reports FAIL for that one bound; it is noise, not a disadvantage. The plan (U8b) names rx as the main metric and CPU beside it; first layout was added to `range.json` as a guard like in `partition.json` and `recheck.json`. With the plan's metrics alone (first layout secondary) `stats.mjs` gives PASS for the lever and both corrections on the same data. Earlier runs before the 304 only updated the headers (39732494439 with 15 runs, 39738419956 with 30 runs on CNN): C rx 0.451 and 0.423, CPU 0.757 and 0.810; run 2 showed C YouTube first visit CPU 1.041 [1.016, 1.066], which run 3 did not repeat (1.000 [0.988, 1.012]). Final engine (CI 39785610279) in CI perf run 39791274655 (same plan): second visit CNN C rx 0.547 [0.482, 0.647], CPU 0.814 [0.707, 0.900], loaded 0.835; S rx 0.608, CPU 0.769 [0.728, 0.815], loaded 0.783; first layout C 1.021 [0.985, 1.077], S 0.972; YouTube within 2 %, memory at most 1.012. That job showed C CNN first visit first layout 1.125 [1.107, 1.158]; all of it lay before the first request of the engine (ready to first request 3.9 against 2.2 s in every run, first request to first layout 0.986 [0.962, 1.015]), where no code of this change runs (on a first visit the loops come 2 to 6 s after the first layout), and three new jobs did not repeat it (CI perf run 39818119406, first layout 1.014, 0.998 and 1.001): a start offset of that one job, so `run.mjs` now waits until every engine is in the code cache of the template. Kept: the lever passes by rule R with the plan metrics, the corrections pass |

Patch 0048 lets the buffer proxy give a new target layer the hole again (WebCore makes a new layer when a hidden video is shown, and the hole punch buffer comes only once). `VideoOverlay.cpp` accepts a hole drawn straight into the framebuffer without opacity, blend mode, color filter or perspective, records its 2D transform and sends changed placements after each shown frame (`place` over `onEngineMedia`, also from the compositor thread); a refused hole draws nothing and the player copies frames into the tiles again for good (#217). Clipping, rounded corners and content above the video come from the hole. Measured locally under SwiftShader (no GPU; a 1280x720 VP9 clip shown at 960x540, 10 s): the renderer process (engine and page) needs 0.2 instead of 0.8 cores, Chromium in total 5.3 instead of 7.2 CPU seconds per second; the rest is SwiftShader compositing in the GPU process (native Chromium 3.3), which bounds both paths on two cores (14 frames per second shown by the host against 13 by the engine, native Chromium 29). Costs: while scrolling the video can lag its hole by a frame, the engine's own snapshots (`pagePixel`, print) lack the video |

| 257 | HTTP authentication (401 with Basic and Digest) | Engine code only, no patch: WebKit's curl `AuthenticationChallenge` has no `AuthenticationClient`, so `ResourceLoader::didReceiveAuthenticationChallenge` cannot be used, and our loads are not `ResourceHandle`s. `ResourceLoad` (`LoaderStrategy.cpp`) handles a 401 with Basic or Digest (`availableHttpAuth`) like `NetworkDataTaskCurl::tryHttpAuthentication`: credentials of the address once (curl sent them as Basic already, so only for Digest), then the stored credential of the protection space (host, port, scheme, realm) unless it was just refused (then it is forgotten), then the user; with credentials the same request goes again with `setUserPass` and the scheme, curl answers the challenge (Digest with its own round trip). At most 5 answers 401 per load, then the page gets the 401. The credentials live in WebCore's `CredentialStorage` of the session (one partition, like Chrome's HTTP auth cache), in memory only; `createCurlRequest` sends the stored Basic credential of a URL's directory ahead (`HttpAuth::applyStoredCredential`), so every loader (downloads, icons, pings, service worker fetches) uses them, but only when the request may carry credentials (`allowCookies`, the same flag that decides cookies) and has neither credentials in the URL nor its own `Authorization` header. Who may ask (`HttpAuth::viewToAsk`): WebCore's `isAllowedToAskUserForCredentials` (stored credentials allowed, credentials mode, same origin as the top document for subresources, fetch/XHR only same origin) plus: frames other than the main frame only same origin with the top document, never worker or service worker loads (`initiatorContext`, destinations), never pages without a view (service and shared worker pages), never requests with their own `Authorization` header (the page handles the 401 itself, as before). Chrome's rule ("third-party sub-content may not ask", policy `AllowCrossOriginAuthPrompt` off by default) is about the same; WebKit compares origins, a bit stricter than Chrome's site. Asking is asynchronous: the load keeps its paused curl request and waits; loads of the same view and protection space share one prompt. Event `auth` `{id, url, origin, realm, scheme, retry}`, answer `bl_auth_answer(id, username, password)` (username null cancels, the waiting loads show their 401), `auth-cancel` `{id}` when every waiting load went away (stopped, navigated, view closed), so the core aborts the handler's signal; abiVersion 12. Core: handler `authenticate(request)` returning `{ username, password }` or null (default null). No proxy flag: the engine has no HTTP proxy (TCP goes through the transport), and Chrome does not accept a 407 from an origin server either. ui: `loginPrompt`, like Chrome's dialog with the origin and the warning for `http:`, without the realm (a text of the server that could pose as the browser; it stays in the request for integrators); the demo uses it. Test: `tests/core/auth-test.mjs` | Open item of the status: pages behind Basic or Digest showed the body of the 401. Engine code keeps the change cheap (no WebKit rebuild) and the asynchronous request fits the `permission` pattern instead of the synchronous `dialog`, so the engine thread never waits for the user |
| 258 | Dialogs hold only their page | `runDialog` (`engine/src/Clients.cpp`) hands the dialog to the host asynchronously and runs a nested event loop of the engine thread until the answer (`runNestedEventLoop` in `EngineLoop.cpp`), like the modal run loop of WebKitLegacy: the mailbox and the local tasks, due RunLoop timers (`TimerBase::fireTimersInNestedEventLoop`), otherwise `Atomics.wait` on the pthread address that Emscripten notifies (at most 10 ms). WebCore's `PageGroupLoadDeferrer` suspends the dialog's page group (timers, tasks, parser); popups with an opener share the opener's page group (Chromium's browsing context group). The loader holds back what arrives for deferred pages and delivers it in order afterwards (`setDefersLoading`). Views of that group drop input, run host calls afterwards in order and make no rendering update (the last frame stays); `bl_try_close` closes them without asking. A view closed during its dialog ends the dialog and is torn down in a later task. Nested work runs inside the waiting script's VM entry: the script watchdog restarts per work item, `JSExecState` starts empty (microtasks after each script), the GC runs when idle at the safepoint of patch 0012, caches are only released afterwards. The core no longer pauses the watchdog, a navigation of the view first answers its dialogs with the default. Dialogs of two views nest: the outer page continues only after the inner dialog | A dialog stopped every view, so the `dialog` handler could not be an in-page dialog without freezing the browser. JSPI or Asyncify to unwind the stack would mean rebuilding all of WebKit and slower code; the nested loop needs no WebKit patch. Without the compositor thread (no WebGL 2) the engine worker presents the frames, which reach the screen only when its JS task ends: other views then show their frames after the dialog. | 270 | Images drawn small decode small (JPEG) | Patch 0054: on this port `BitmapImageDescriptor` computes WebKit's subsampling level from the drawn scale like Cocoa, but rounded down (`floor(log2(1 / scale))`, so the decoded image keeps at least the drawn pixels and stays sharp; Cocoa rounds up and subsamples only above 5 MP), capped by what the decoder can do (`frameSizeAtIndex` per level). `JPEGImageDecoder` decodes at 1/2, 1/4 or 1/8 with libjpeg-turbo's DCT scaling (`scale_denom`) and starts over with a new reader when the level changes; other decoders report the full size for every level and are unchanged. WebKit's `ImageFrame` cache already keeps the level: a frame of a lower level serves higher ones, a larger draw (page zoom, pinch zoom, canvas `drawImage` at full size, `getImageData` afterwards) decodes again. `ImageSubsamplingEnabled` on (`engine/src/WebView.cpp`), `BL_IMAGE_SUBSAMPLING=0` (expert) switches it off | Phones and 32 bit hosts have little engine memory, iOS Safari ends tabs early. Against Chromium's rendering of the same page the picture got closer (PSNR 42.5 instead of 29.5 dB at 400 x 300, 36.9 instead of 34.6 dB at page zoom 4): the DCT scaling filters better than Skia's downscale of the full image. Like Safari, a canvas `drawImage` into a small canvas also uses the smaller decoding. Open: PNG, WebP, AVIF; `img.decode()` decodes at full size. | 283 | Network fingerprint of Safari 26 | The engine sends Safari 26's TLS ClientHello as far as OpenSSL allows (patch 0060, `NetworkFingerprintWasm.cpp`): its cipher suites incl. the three 3DES ones (OpenSSL with `enable-weak-ssl-ciphers`), ChaCha20 kept first (#65); groups X25519MLKEM768, X25519, P-256, P-384, P-521 with key shares for the first two; its signature algorithms with the duplicate `rsa_pss_rsae_sha384` and `rsa_pkcs1_sha1` (offered through a security callback, a SHA-1 server signature is still refused); `status_request`, SCT (permissive) and zlib certificate compression (OpenSSL with zlib); no `encrypt_then_mac` and `post_handshake_auth`. Request headers in Safari's order (measured: one order up to 6 headers, one from 7 on, then `Accept-Language`, `Priority`, `Accept-Encoding`, `Cookie`, `Connection`), `Accept-Language` with qualities (`en-US,en;q=0.9`), `Priority` with the urgency of the load priority (`u=0` documents to `u=7` beacons), `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br, zstd` | Bot managers (Akamai, Cloudflare, DataDome, HUMAN) compare TLS (JA3, JA4) and HTTP/2 (Akamai fingerprint) with the user agent; the engine claimed Safari but sent curl's fingerprint. Hidden, so the profile sync (`validName`) leaves it out and a deleted IndexedDB directory with only its log counts as gone. V8 compiles `CLoop::execute` (186 KB) with TurboFan, but it is no faster than Liftoff (`--no-liftoff` 277-290 against 278-311 ms): 28 % of its instructions access spill slots, the registers of the C loop are live through the whole function. A toy interpreter with that register pressure (12 live registers, 300 handlers) was slower as one function per handler with tail calls (about 60 ms) than as a switch (35-41 ms): passing the registers on every tail call costs more than the spills. The control flow of the C loop is fine (one 823-way `br_table`, no shadow stack). Integer `%` has no fast path on 32 bit (`slow_path_mod` + `fmod`), but costs 0.4 % on weather.com | Tail calls would also have required Safari 18.2. | 446 | Full database writes on a storage thread, in pieces of 1 MB (changes #180) | `engine/src/MemoryVFS.cpp`: the write about 1 s after a change and `bl_storage_flush` run on a thread of their own (`storageQueue`, a WTF `WorkQueue` created with the VFS), not on the engine thread. Under the lock of the database it only takes a copy: `Content` blocks are shared and copied before a write (copy on write, `Content::share`), so SQLite goes on reading and committing while the file is written. The file is written from the latest version of each page in pieces of 1 MB with its hash in the same pass, no longer every committed WAL frame on its own. The I/O lock is per database; a commit does not wait for it, whoever holds it appends the records waiting meanwhile (`IOLock`), all waiting records in one write. Closing the last connection still writes on the closing thread. `BL_OPFS_WRITE_DELAY_MS` (only with `BL_TEST_HOOKS=1`) makes every OPFS write slower for tests | On edition.cnn.com no pointer move reached the page for 3 to 13 s (6 of 12 runs): the engine thread waited in `writeAtOffset` for up to about 1000 writes of 4 KB, one per WAL frame of a 24 KB cookie database (in Chrome's incognito every OPFS write is an IPC to the browser process). Locally (loaded machine, 160 moves) 2 stalls of 4.5 s and 28 lost moves before, none after. A commit made during a full write reaches the log right after it instead of before the commit returns. Test: `storage-flush-test.mjs` (with 50 ms per write the longest timer gap was 29.8 s, now under 30 ms; all 150 commits there after a restart) |

| 475 | A memory release and a view that leaves its page empty JSC's code cache (corrects #441) | Patch 0102: `JSC::clearCodeCache(VM&)` (new private header `runtime/CodeCacheRelease.h`) empties the VM's `CodeCache` once no JS is on the stack; `WebCore::releaseMemory` calls it with `MaintainJSCode::Yes` (with `No` it deletes all code anyway). `engine/src/MemoryWatch.cpp` `pageLeft()` calls it when a main frame commits a new document (`WebView::didCommitLoad`) and when a view closes. JSC's `CodeCache`, keyed by the source text, holds the unlinked bytecode of every program, module, eval and `Function` code evaluated with strong references, through it the bytecode of the functions of those scripts that ran and the script sources (with them the `CachedScript`s in the memory cache), and prunes only when a new script comes; before #441 every release deleted it with all other code. 6 pages of 3000 functions left behind 14 MB of JS heap and 35 MB of malloc after a full GC; deleting the linked code alone freed nothing, only `deleteAllCode` did. Real sites (16 heavy page loads in one tab, then about:blank and a full GC, local Chromium, both engines under the same load): 474 to 525 MB in use instead of 573 to 619, JS heap 39 to 61 MB instead of 71 to 79, scripts in the memory cache 9 to 28 MB instead of 38 to 42, each release frees 105 to 206 MB instead of 54 to 115. | 478 | Host layers: the host animates layers that only animate their opacity (adds to #454) | Patch 0104: `SkiaCompositingLayer` lists in `prepareFrame` (with a frame damage) every leaf without effects (opacity 1, filter, mask, replica, blend mode, backdrop, 3D) under ancestors without effects, drawn axis aligned, whose only animation runs on opacity, with its clipped rect in device pixels and whether a layer drawn after it overlaps it; a layer marked `setDrawnByEmbedder` is left out of the frame and its opacity damages nothing, `drawEmbedderLayer` draws it alone at opacity 1 with the clips of its ancestors, `opacityAt` gives its animation's opacity at a running time. `engine/src/HostLayers.cpp`: a listed layer that stood still for 20 frames is asked of the host with the view event `layers` (abiVersion 27) with its place in frame pixels, its animation's start (ms since 1970), duration of two iterations, iterations and its opacity sampled 30 times a second plus where the line between two samples misses by more than 0.004 (steps); the core (`view-host-layers.ts`) puts a canvas there above the damage tiles (`z-index`, the tile layer isolated), runs the samples as a Web Animation with that start time and transfers the canvas to the presenter worker (`{ blLayer, viewId, key }`). Once it arrived, a frame draws the view without the layer and the layer into its canvas in the same worker task; it goes back into the frames, its canvas emptied in the same task, as soon as its rect or clip changes, a later layer overlaps it, the engine's opacity differs from the samples by more than 0.02 or the start by 20 ms (paused, replaced), its animation ends, or the view gets a new size or a pinch; new contents are drawn into its canvas. At most 64 per view. The compositor's context has alpha with host layers. `BL_HOST_LAYERS=0` keeps them in the frames | Finding of 2026-11-09: a page of 46 pulsing skeleton boxes (the loading state of news and shop pages) cost 7.5 to 7.9 CPU cores under SwiftShader at 60 frames per second, plain Chromium 0.07: the boxes damage most of the view, so every frame was a whole view canvas, which the host repaints whole (#454), and Chromium runs such animations on its compositor without any repaint. Measured in plain Chromium first (1280x800, SwiftShader): a whole view WebGL canvas cleared every frame 3.8 to 4.5 cores, 46 canvases repainted every frame 2.4, 46 static canvases with a CSS opacity animation 0.10. With the engine (local, demo, 20 s standing still, the same build A/B): 0.14 to 0.16 cores and no engine frame against 5.96 to 6.67 cores at 52 to 55 frames per second with `BL_HOST_LAYERS=0`; the engine before 4.6 to 5.0 cores at 40 to 43; plain Chromium 0.08. Answers the objection of #454 against host elements: the host sets the place only while the canvas is empty, and what the engine shows goes into the canvases from the worker in the same task as the view frame, as with the tiles. Sampling instead of translating WebKit's timing functions into CSS keeps the host on the engine's curve exactly (also its order of animation and keyframe timing functions). Not taken: transform animations (the host would scale a fixed bitmap, the clip changes every frame; Wikipedia's pulsating dot scales and fades and stays in damage tiles), moving host layers while the page scrolls (out of step with the worker's frames). Test: `host-layers-test.mjs` (ratio 1 and 2, Chromium and Firefox: no frames while 6 boxes pulse, the covered one stays in the frames, what the host layers show matches the view after pausing within 3 levels, scrolling and new contents; the engine before fails it) |
| 479 | A new HTTP/2 connection takes more requests only after the server announced its stream limit | curl patch `deps/patches/curl/0003`: the HTTP/2 filter answers the new connection filter query `CF_QUERY_LIMIT_PENDING` until the server's first SETTINGS arrive, which also wake the waiting transfers (`Curl_multi_connchanged`). Meanwhile `url_match_multiplex_limits` (`lib/url.c`) lets a transfer with `PIPEWAIT` (WebCore sets it for HTTPS) wait on that connection like on one whose HTTP version is not known yet, instead of taking it or opening another one. Then the waiting transfers take what the limit leaves and the rest open the next connection (6 per host, #176) as before | curl assumed 100 concurrent streams until the SETTINGS, so a new connection took every waiting request at once. www.postfinance.ch (Envoy) allows 10 and resets the streams over that with `FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR`, which neither curl nor Chrome repeat (curl error 92): stylesheets and scripts of the page failed, the main navigation fell apart and the cookie dialog was missing; at other times the server closed such a connection 80 ms after it opened. Reproduced without the engine: 36 requests with Node's HTTP/2 client before the SETTINGS, 12 or 13 reset, after them none. The first connection of a host carries only the navigation, the flood came on the second one that curl opens when the first is full. Real servers send their SETTINGS with the last flight of the handshake (4 to 12 ms after it on 8 sites, under one round trip), so the wait costs nothing there; a server that sends them only after the handshake (Node) delays the other requests of a new connection by up to one round trip (24 requests to a new origin at 150 ms: 1.91 to 2.00 s instead of 1.74 to 1.88 s). Respecting the limit costs postfinance some parallelism it used to get when the server let the flood pass (load 4.6 to 4.9 s instead of 4.1 to 4.6 s locally), but no request fails any more. Not chosen: repeating reset streams (the server would see the flood again, `FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR` does not say the request was not processed), a lower assumed limit for every connection (less parallelism on servers that allow 100), waiting for a free stream on the full connection like Chrome instead of opening another one (measured no faster on postfinance, and curl wakes one waiting transfer per change, which could stall them behind transfers of other hosts). `tests/core/http2-stream-limit-test.mjs`: a server that allows 4 streams and resets like Envoy, 24 requests at once (before 8 of 24 loaded and 16 streams reset, after 24 and none) |
| 480 | `:visited` from the engine's own visited link store, filled by the views and the host's history | `engine/src/VisitedLinks.cpp`: one `WebCore::VisitedLinkStore` for all views (set in the page configuration of `WebView`, which had WebCore's empty store that answers "not visited" for every link), a set of the shared string hashes of the visited addresses like the stores of WebKitLegacy and WebKit2. WebCore's `HistoryController` adds every address a frame commits; a new hash restyles the links to it in the open documents (`invalidateStylesForLink`), clearing restyles all links. New exports `bl_add_visited_links(urls)` (newline separated) and `bl_clear_visited_links()`, abiVersion 28; core `browser.addVisitedLinks(urls)` and `browser.clearVisitedLinks()`. The demo passes its history (`history.ts`, this device only) at every start, also after a restart, and clears the store with the history. Not partitioned by site, like Safari and Firefox; WebCore limits `:visited` to colors and reports the unvisited style to scripts. `engine/hot-functions.txt` marks the store's code hot (`tools/hot-functions.mjs --add`): the empty store's destructor was cold, so every start waited for a chunk. Tests: `tests/core/visited-test.mjs` (loaded and added addresses, restyle of the open page and of a page from the back/forward cache, clearing, `getComputedStyle`), `tests/demo/ui-test.mjs` (history after a restart) | Visited links kept the unvisited color everywhere (search results, Wikipedia), unlike every browser. The engine alone forgets its visits at every restart, and the host owns the history the user sees and clears, so the host fills the store. Hashes instead of addresses keep the memory small (4 bytes per address) and nothing readable in the engine; partitioning like Chrome would need the top site and frame origin of every visit and of every link check, which WebCore's store interface does not carry |

| 500 | The machine stack probe is a frame, and it holds for at most 64 checks (adds to #354, #374) | `engine/jit/MachineStack.cpp`: the recursion checks probe with a WASM function whose frame is 94 KB (12032 loaded values live across a call on a path it never takes), so only its prologue runs, which checks the stack pointer minus the frame against the browser's limit: about 0.1 instead of 8 us. Made at the first probe and used once a test in a later task, from a shallow stack, showed that it fails where the recursion probe fails for 90 % of its room; until then, where the test fails and for the JIT, the recursion probe (now in bytes: 94 and 214 KB, the 300 and 700 frames before). A probe holds for at most 64 checks (256 with the recursion probe) and while the shadow stack stays within 4 KB below and 16 KB above its point (64 KB before). Test: `tests/core/stack-test.mjs` (a 100000 long `===` chain, `||` chains after a deeper one) | Two engine crashes found with the parser work (#499): the code of `x === x === ...` recursed about 110 bytes of machine stack per level without any shadow stack, so no probe ran and V8 stopped the engine at 4518 levels; and a deep `||` chain after a deeper one in the same task passed on the first one's probe although V8 had optimized the function in between (140 instead of 102 bytes per level), crashing at 3516 levels. Counting checks needs cheap probes: every 128 checks with the recursion probe cost 0.5 to 1.5 s in 30 s loads of weather.com, YouTube, Reddit and Walmart (57 to 176 thousand probes, 2 to 5 %). With the frame Walmart makes 389 thousand probes in 30 s; sort with a comparator, a `JSON.parse` reviver, getters and code generation take the same time as before (A/B, best of 8). The window of 16 KB probes as often as 64 KB on those loads, 8 KB twice as often. In workers a frame of 88 KB failed where the recursion did in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, one of 200 KB not in WebKit and nested frames of 8 KB not in Firefox, hence the test before trusting it; the engine trusts it in Chromium and Firefox. Not taken: a lower reserve (the depth of getter chains would grow by about 7 %), a stack of its own for deep work (JSPI exists only in Chromium and runs asynchronously) |
| 501 | The watchdog stops a script only when it keeps the host waiting (changes #146, #333) | `engine/src/EngineLoop.cpp`: the core writes into a uint32 of the engine (`bl_host_wait_address`, abiVersion 33) whether it waits for an answer of the engine thread: 0, or a new number each time input or a call starts to wait (`packages/core/src/watchdog.ts`), and anew on `keepWaiting()` and after a native dialog of the host page. JSC's watchdog looks every 0.5 s while an entry into JS runs (its callback starts the timer again, JSC does not when the callback declines); once the same wait has lasted `scriptTimeoutSeconds` (default 30) it terminates the entry as before (uncatchable, a console message), and the next script gets the whole limit again. A script nobody waits for runs to its end. So the demo's "Wait" now gives the script the limit again ("Unless you wait, the engine stops it"). Tests: `tests/core/background-test.mjs` (limit 3 s: a script of 6 s nobody waits for ends normally, one that keeps a click waiting is stopped 3.6 s after the click; limit 20 s: after `keepWaiting()` the script is stopped 21 s later, not 20 s after the click; all four failed before), `tests/demo/ui-test.mjs`, `tests/engine/pages/hang.html` (`render.mjs` waits from the start) | Cloudflare's Turnstile (OpenStreetMap's sign-up) runs single entries of 9 to 23 s here (plain Chromium: at most about 1 s), 40 s at a load of 28, and the old limit of 30 s per entry terminated its VM: the widget stayed at "Verifying... Stuck?". On a slower phone such entries take longer still. Local A/B on the sign-up page with a limit of 5 s standing in for a slower device (3 runs each, alternating): before 6 terminations per run and the widget stuck in 3 of 3, after none and the checkbox in 3 of 3 (with 10 s: 2 of 3 runs terminated, 1 stuck, after none). Chrome stops no script on its own, it asks once input goes unanswered; the limit per entry also stopped a script after the user chose "Wait". What stays different from Chrome: all views share the engine thread, so a script nobody waits for holds the other tabs (timers, animations, video) until the user does something; then the input waits and the limit applies. Not taken: a longer fixed limit (any limit fails a slow enough device), a limit only for cross-origin frames (the challenge is one, but so are ads that loop) |

| 503 | `navigator.mediaCapabilities` with the host's answers | `engine/src/media/MediaCapabilitiesWasm.cpp` installs a factory into WebCore's `PlatformMediaEngineConfigurationFactory` (no patch: `installFactory` is public). `decodingInfo` (pages and dedicated workers, WebCore asks on the engine thread for both) first checks what the engine does itself, `MediaPlayerPrivateWasm::takesType` (the media feature, for MSE the fMP4 and WebM containers of its parsers; also the first step of `canPlayType` and `isTypeSupported` now), and then asks the host's own `decodingInfo` with the same configuration asynchronously on the host's main thread, where its `<video>` and MediaSource live; `supported`, `smooth` and `powerEfficient` are the host's answer (`bl__media_capabilities`, back on the engine thread). | 512 | A service worker that does not stop is given up, not the engine's process | Patch 0111: `SWContextManager::serviceWorkerFailedToTerminate` (10 s after `terminateWorker`) no longer calls `terminateProcess` on this port; it records the worker's `ServiceWorkerThreadProxy` as given up, drops its termination request and reports `workerTerminated`, so the server counts the worker as ended and runs it again on its next event (same identifier, new thread). Test: `tests/engine/pages/cssom-rules.html` (33 changes, also in a shadow tree, an adopted sheet, with `:has()`, pseudo-elements and custom properties, give the computed style of a fresh style; next to 4000 elements and a web font an inserted rule costs 2 ms against 64 ms for a full restyle, before 53 against 47 ms) | blick.ch's styled-components insert about 300 rules on an in-site navigation. More than half of it came from the fonts: a face made anew for an unchanged rule found its font in the memory cache, reported it loaded, and `fontsNeedUpdate` scheduled a full style rebuild, which every new resolver of a page with web fonts did (also after each automatic memory release, #61). Building the resolver itself stays (10 to 15 ms, 6 % of the engine thread during the tap): WebKit's rule sets span all sheets, an update of only the changed sheet would need rule sets per sheet like Blink's |
| 531 | Hidden views that wait for the user start nothing at all: their style, layout, load events and parser wait too (adds to #364) | Patch 0125: `Page::setDefersStyleAndLayout`. A script that asks for style or layout and the rendering update of a shown view get them as before. A stylesheet, an image or a DOM change shortly before the click thus started tasks of seconds after the hold had begun: Wikipedia's World War II article restyled for 1.5 s and laid itself out for 1.4 s, CNN's image `onload` forced a layout of 2.4 s, the parsers of CNN and Spiegel ran chunks for 3 s between the keys. Locally with Wikipedia, CNN and Spiegel in background tabs, 40 keys 40 ms apart at 5 to 60 s and a machine load of 16 to 28, the worst rounds had median 220 to 584 ms, 90th percentile up to 1.4 s and up to 1.5 s; now every round has median under 12 ms, 90th percentile under 52 ms, at most 228 ms, and the engine steps over 50 ms during the holds went from up to 37 (up to 1.8 s) to 3 (at most 120 ms). | 543 | Engine workers whose `engine.js` did not load are replaced, one at a time | `engine/js/workers.js` `blWatchWorkerLoads`, run through Emscripten's `preInit` right after the pool of 8 workers is created: an `error` event of a worker that is no `ErrorEvent` (its script did not load) no longer reaches Emscripten's handler, which threw the event while the load never ended. | 547 | `performance.timeOrigin` is converted to the wall clock once | Patch 0134: `Performance` (`Source/WebCore/page/Performance.cpp`) keeps `m_wallTimeOrigin`, the time origin as wall clock time taken when the object is made (like `m_continuousTimeOrigin` next to it), and `timeOrigin()` reduces that instead of converting the monotonic origin at every read; for all ports, since the time origin is one moment by the spec. `tests/engine/pages/time-origin.html` reads it 20000 times in a page, a frame and a worker | WebKit's conversion (origin minus monotonic now plus wall now) takes two clocks at every read; the build's wall clock is `Date.now()` in whole milliseconds against a microsecond monotonic clock, so the value moved between two neighbouring milliseconds (two values in every realm in the test, before). Chrome, Firefox and Safari give one value, and bot checks see the difference (found while looking at YouTube's stream protection, which this did not settle, [status](status.md)). Not taken: a finer wall clock for the build (`performance.timeOrigin + performance.now()` of the host), which would also move `Date.now()` away from the host's clock after a suspend |
| 548 | Text fields of the settings save an edit before their section goes away | `settings-page.ts`: a text field keeps its saved value as `defaultValue`; before the dialog's content is cleared (close, another section) every text field whose value differs gets its `change` sent, so the save no longer depends on the browser's own `change`. Not taken: saving at every `input`, which would write the profile at every key and check a half typed time zone |
| 549 | The engine plays no HLS playlist and no DASH manifest as a file | `MediaPlayerPrivateWasm::takesType` (`engine/src/media/MediaPlayerPrivateWasm.cpp`) refuses streaming manifests for file playback whatever the host answers (`isStreamingManifest`: the subtypes `vnd.apple.mpegurl`, `mpegurl` and `x-mpegurl` of any type, `application/dash+xml`), so `canPlayType` answers "" and `decodingInfo` of type `file` unsupported; the host is no longer asked ahead for these types. Players that ask `canPlayType` first now stream through MSE: SRF's player (video.js VHS) plays with the Android identity (31.9 s after 32 s, local); with a Safari user agent VHS keeps Safari's native `audioTracks` and stops after the first segment (3.9 s) on streams with a separate audio rendition, as in plain Chromium with a Safari user agent and `audioTracks` on ([status](status.md), Open: features). | 550 | Damage tiles stay on next to videos the host shows (adds to #454 and #232) | `VideoOverlay.cpp` records with each whole frame shown the device pixels every video hole clears in the view (the hole's bounds through its transform, one pixel more for antialiased edges, clipped to the canvas's clip), and `holes(viewId)` gives them to the compositor, which adds them to the areas a damage tile must not overlap (`rectsDrawnWhole` of `DamageTiles::tilesFor`). A hole punched into a tile makes the frame whole only where it clears pixels of that tile: the compositor walks every layer for each tile, so before any video in the layer tree counted. Replaces the rule that a frame is whole as long as the last whole frame placed any video. Test: `tests/core/damage-tiles-test.mjs` (a spinner next to a playing and a paused video and one far below the view turns in its tiles while the view canvas keeps its frame; over the video it turns in whole frames; a hidden video's hole closes with a whole frame; the old engine fails the first). `safari-test.mjs` looks for the field arrows of iOS as the nearest focusable elements around the IME field: with tiles next to the playing video there, the layer of the tiles lies between the IME field and the next arrow, which tab order skips | Any video with a host placement turned the damage tiles off for the whole view, also paused or 4000 px below it, so a small animation presented a whole view canvas every frame. Measured locally (2026-11-14, Chromium with SwiftShader, 1280x800, packaged demo, a 48 px spinner, 20 s windows, two runs per arm, same core): with a paused video elsewhere 0.92 to 0.93 CPU cores instead of 6.79 (GPU process per frame 12 instead of 110 ms, the spinner alone 0.96), a playing one elsewhere 1.42 to 1.63 instead of 7.37, a playing one below the view 1.95 to 2.12 instead of 7.07 to 7.37 (the host still decodes and composites that video); idle edition.cnn.com 4.4 instead of 7.4 cores, with its ad placeholders stopped 2.5 instead of 7.7 (GPU process 1.0 instead of 6.1). A tile on a hole stays a whole frame (spinner over the video 7.1 cores before and after): the tile shows the view canvas beneath through its own hole, and there the view canvas holds the content of the last whole frame over the video (the old spinner), and placements change only with whole frames. Not taken: tiles over unchanged holes with the view canvas cleared under them (a whole frame once, then tiles), more state across frames for an animation that rarely sits on a playing video |
| 551 | A RunLoop step of the engine has no time budget; the debug log walks the JS heap only after a long GC | A step (`pumpRunLoop`, `EngineLoop.cpp`) still runs one cycle of WTF's RunLoop and returns early only for input and the compositor's answers (#347, #359). With `log=1` `runWithGCAfterwards` no longer measures `Heap::size()` before and after every work item, only after a GC of more than 5 ms ("GC 11 ms, Heap 72137 kB after") | Long steps on heavy sites (netflix.com, webflow.com, ricardo.ch, 2026-11-14) were thought to be many tasks run back to back. A build that counted the items of each step (due timers and functions, a hook after each in patch form) showed each step of 1 s and more to be one item of nearly its length (ricardo 4.6 s of a 4.65 s step of 30 items, webflow 2.3 s of 2.4 s, netflix 1.4 of 1.5 s). The same build ended a step after 50 ms between two items (also WebCore's loops over scripts): on ricardo (5 runs each) the longest step 3.1 to 3.6 s instead of 3.3 to 5.1 s, but rendering updates 20 % more time and the load 1 s later on average; netflix and webflow unchanged. A tap sends no double click (each tap is click count 1), so phones are unchanged. Not taken: K, J and L (YouTube's own keys, not Chrome's), a delay before the first click acts (Chrome's tap timer) |

| 554 | An intersection observer computes its observations again only after something may have moved a target, like Blink | Patch 0137: `IntersectionObserver::geometryVersion()`, a counter of the main thread, goes up at every style update (`Document::resolveStyle`), layout (`LocalFrameViewLayoutContext::performLayout`), scroll of a frame (`LocalFrameView::scrollPositionChanged`) or a scroller (`RenderLayerScrollableArea::scrollTo`), change of the layout or visual viewport, the obscured insets, the page scale or the scroll adjustment of an anchored box, at every `observe()` and when a document is fully active again. `updateObservations` returns without work while the counter is where it was at its last run; a style update or layout that changes nothing still counts | WebKit computed every target of every observer in every rendering update.
